Let's understand the LP approach: For current f (length n, nonnegative, sum sqrt(2n)), M = max(f*f). Solve LP: variables b_j >=0, maximize sum b_j subject to (f*b)_m <= M for all m=0..2n-2. Then rescale g to same sum as f, update f = (1-t) f + t g. Repeating nonincreasing ||f*f||∞. Why? For f fixed, maximize l1 norm of g under f*g∞≤M. If optimum sum larger than current sum (normalized?), then new f with larger sum for same M reduces M after renormalization. It is like expanding along flat directions. This is reminiscent of "sequential quadratic" for minimizing max convolution. Could implement with scipy linprog. Variables b length n. Constraints: convolution(f,b)_m <= M for all 2n-1. b>=0. Since f,b nonnegative, constraints nonnegative. Maximize sum b. We can solve many LPs. Each LP has 2n-1 constraints and n variables. For n=2535, constraints~5069, variables2535. LP with scipy HiGHS maybe okay for few iterations? 1800s. Need optimize speed.

Implementation of `max_conv_fast(a)`:
```
def conv_max(a):
    n=a.size
    if n<=32: return np.max(np.convolve(a,a))
    N=1
    while N < 2*n-1: N <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)
    return float(np.max(c[:2*n-1]))
```
Potential roundoff negative small; okay. For dense direct maybe exact? Use direct for final validation maybe. For line search tolerance, use fast. For n small, direct.

Feasibility check for h: `max_conv_fast(h) <= 1.0 + tol` and all h>=0. We may want margin: allow <=1.000000001. After rescale by sqrt(max), if max slightly >1 due roundoff, divide.

Line search bisection:
```
def largest_feasible_alpha(f,g):
    Sg=np.sum(g); Sf=np.sum(f)
    if Sg <= Sf: return 0.0
    # if g itself feasible? maybe not
    lo=0.0; hi=1.0
    if conv_max(f + hi*(g-f)) <= 1+tol: return hi
    # if mid infeasible, hi=mid; else lo=mid
    for _ in range(30):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       h=f+mid*(g-f)
       if conv_max(h) <= 1+tol: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    return lo
```
30 iterations enough. Each FFT for n=1000 cheap. But LP already heavy. Could reduce to 20.
Alternatively use step doubling from 0.5. But okay.

score_est = 2*n/(S*S) if M=1
```

- `conv_max_fast(a, tol=0.0)`: using direct for small n else FFT. Must handle a as numpy float64 1D.
```
def conv_max_fast(a):
    n=a.size
    if n<2: return float(a[0]*a[0])
    if n<=256:
        return float(np.max(np.convolve(a,a)))
    m=2*n-1
    N=1
    while N < m: N <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fa*=fa
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa, N)
    return float(np.max(c[:m]))
```
FFT roundoff can produce max slightly less than true; for feasibility we can use direct for n<=2048? Direct n=2048 ~4M conv? Actually np.convolve direct O(n^2)? For 2048, ~4M terms? def get_conv_pattern(n):
    if n in cache: return cache[n]
    m=2*n-1
    rows=[]; cols=[]; fidx=[]
    for k in range(m):
       start=max(0,k-(n-1))
       end=min(n-1,k)
       if start>end: continue
       # for i in start..end: row k, col i, f_idx = k-i
       cols.extend(range(start,end+1))
       fidx.extend(np.arange(k-start? wait f_idx = k - np.arange(start,end+1)))
```

    pos+=cnt
```

LP solve function:
```
def solve_lp_for_g(f, pattern, time_limit_s=30.0):
    n = f.size
    m = pattern['m']
    data = f[pattern['fidx']].astype(np.float64)
    A = csr_matrix((data, pattern['col'], pattern['indptr']), shape=(m, n))
    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(m), bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': time_limit})
    if res.status == 0 and res.x is not None:
        g = np.maximum(res.x,0)
        # Due numerical, ensure f*g <=1? maybe okay.
        return g
    return None
```
`bounds=[(0,None)]*n` creates list repeated okay but no closure. Use tuple? `bounds=(0, None)` applies same to all variables? linprog accepts `bounds=[(0, None)]*n` or `bounds=(0, None)`. In scipy linprog, `bounds` can be `[(0, None)]*n`. Use that.
set `options` maybe: `{'presolve': True, 'primal_feasibility_tolerance': 1e-9, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance': 1e-9, 'time_limit': remaining}`. The time_limit option in HiGHS? `time_limit` accepted? For scipy optimize linprog HiGHS, options include `time_limit`. We can pass max(1, remaining LP budget). If time limit interrupts, status 3? fallback.

Let's implement `line_search_direction` more concretely:
```
def direction_score(f, alpha, g):
    h=f + alpha*(g-f)
    if np.any(h<0): h=np.maximum(h,0)
    S=np.sum(h)
    if S <= 0: return inf
    M=conv_max_fast(h)
    return 2.0*h.size*M/(S*S)
```

Now, random perturbations:
```
def random_perturb(f, rng, scale=0.1):
    n=f.size
    logf=np.log(np.maximum(f,1e-300))
    widths = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//20)))
    start=int(rng.integers(0,n-widths))
    logf[start:start+widths] += scale*rng.normal(size=widths)
    cand=np.exp(logf)
    # optional sym with prob 0.2
    if rng.random()<0.25: cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
    return normalize_m1(cand)
```
If score fast smaller, update f. This uses nonnegative. Use when no LP improvement: e.g., 20 random attempts per budget chunk.

```
def price_conv(a, p):
   n=a.size
   rev=a[::-1]
   N=next_pow2(3*n-1)
   fr=np.fft.rfft(rev,N); fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fr*fp,N)
   return c[n-1:n-1+n]
```
This is useful for gradient.

Detailed code plan:
```
import numpy as np
import time
try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    HAVE SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE SCIPY = False

TARGET = 1.5027
_pattern_cache = {}

def _release_pattern(n):
    _pattern_cache.pop(n, None)

def next_pow2(x):
    r=1
    while r<x: r <<=1
    return r

def conv_max_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return 0.0
    if n==1: return float(a[0]*a[0])
    # For small use direct for accuracy? maybe n<=512 direct to avoid FFT error and faster? np.convolve direct O(n^2) for512=262k, okay. For line search many, FFT better if >128.
    if n <= 256:
        return float(np.max(np.convolve(a,a)))
    m=2*n-1
    N=1
    while N < m: N <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    # roundoff: take max, maybe ensure nonnegative by max with 0
    val=float(np.max(c[:m]))
    if val < 0: return 0.0
    return val
```
For feasibility, maybe if n<=1024 direct? Direct np.convolve in C for 1024 ~1M multiply; line search 30* iterations maybe okay. FFT also fine. Use direct for accuracy in normalization if n<=512? For larger fast.

`eval_fast_score(a)`:
```
def fast_score(a):
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 1e-300: return np.inf
    return 2.0*a.size*conv_max_fast(a)/(s*s)
```

`make_prefix(n)`:
```
def make_prefix(n):
   if n<=0: return np.array([])
   a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
   for k in range(n-1): a[k+1]=a[k]*(2*k+1)/(2*k+2)
   return a
```

If linprog raises, return None.
```
def solve_lp(f, pat, time_limit):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY: return None
    n=f.size
    data=f[pat['fidx']].astype(np.float64, copy=False)
    A=csr_matrix((data, pat['col'], pat['indptr']), shape=(pat['m'], n))
    cobj=-np.ones(n)
    b=np.ones(pat['m'])
    bounds=[(0.0, None)]*n
    try:
       res=linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': max(1.0,time_limit)})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res.status == 0:
       g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
       # clean tiny negatives
       g=np.where(g>0, g, 0.0)
       # Check finite
       if np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
          return g
    return None
```
Time limit option expects float? Use `float(time_limit)`. If status 3 (time limit) maybe res.x None.
Potential issue: `bounds=[(0.0, None)]*n` creates list of same tuple okay.

```
def random_step(f, rng):
    n=f.size
    # choose perturbation type
    r=rng.random()
    if r<0.5:
        # multiplicative block
        logf=np.log(np.maximum(f,1e-300))
        width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//20)))
        start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
        scale=float(rng.normal(0.0,0.15))
        logf[start:start+width] += scale
        cand=np.exp(logf)
    elif r<0.7:
        # smooth cosine perturbation
        logf=np.log(np.maximum(f,1e-300))
        width=int(rng.integers(max(8,n//50), max(16,n//5)))
        start=int(rng.integers(max(0,n//2), max(0,n-width+1)))
        t=np.linspace(0, np.pi, width)
        amp=float(rng.normal(0,0.2))
        logf[start:start+width] += amp*np.sin(t)
        cand=np.exp(logf)
    else:
        cand=f.copy()
        # symmetrize or reversal blend
        cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
    cand=np.maximum(cand,0.0)
    return scale_to_m1(cand)[0]
```

Now, implement `optimize_length` in detail with no nested functions:
```
def optimize_length(n, deadline, start=None, do_lp=True, max_lp_iters=1000):
    if n <= 1: return None, np.inf
    f = make_prefix(n)
    if start is not None and len(start)>0:
       f = np.maximum(np.asarray(start,dtype=float),0)
       # maybe resize to n? If start length n; if not, no.
    f, m = scale_to_m1(f)
    best_f = f.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(f)
    print(...)
    pattern = None
    if do_lp and HAVE_SCIPY and n <= LP_NMAX:
       try: pattern=get_pattern(n)
       except: pattern=None
    rng=np.random.default_rng(12345 + n)
    no_improve=0
    it=0
    lp_fail=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # If best_score <= TARGET - 1e-9: break maybe (but target exact? fast)
       if best_score <= TARGET + 0.0001: # even fast, maybe final exact
           break
       # LP attempt if pattern and time remaining enough
       if pattern is not None and (deadline - time.monotonic()) > 20:
           time_left_lp = min(60.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 10.0)
           g = solve_lp(f, pattern, time_left_lp)
           if g is None:
              lp_fail +=1
              if lp_fail >3:
                  # discard pattern to avoid slow LP
                  _release_pattern(n)
                  pattern=None
                  continue
           else:
              lp_fail=0
           if g is not None:
               sg=float(np.sum(g)); sf=float(np.sum(f))
               if sg > sf*(1.0+1e-8):
                  newf, news = line_search(f,g, min(deadline, time.monotonic()+30))
                  if newf is not None and news < best_score*(1.0-1e-10):
                      best_f=newf.copy(); best_score=news; f=newf
                      no_improve=0
                      it+=1
                      if it%10==0:
                         print(f"n={n} iter {it} score {best_score:.7f}")
                      continue
               # if no sum gain, maybe try sym?
       # If no improvement by LP or no LP, do random/blend
       cand = random_candidate(f, rng)
       newf, news = blend_search(f, cand, deadline)
       if newf is not None and news < best_score*(1.0-1e-10):
           best_f=newf.copy(); best_score=news; f=newf
           no_improve=0
           it+=1
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           # if many no improves, try sym and accept if better
           symf, _ = scale_to_m1(0.5*(f+f[::-1]))
           syms=fast_score(symf)
           if syms < best_score*(1.0-1e-10):
              best_f=symf.copy(); best_score=syms; f=symf
              no_improve=0
           elif no_improve > 200:
              # move to next length? We can break if time per fperturb? But maybe try larger random scale
              if no_improve > 1000:
                  break
    return best_f, best_score
```

```
def blend_search(f,cand,deadline):
   delta=cand-f
   cur=fast_score(f)
   best_a=0.0; best_s=cur
   alphas=np.array([0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0])
   for a in alphas:
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       s=direction_score(f,delta,a)
       if s < best_s:
           best_s=s; best_a=a
   if best_a==0: return None,cur
   h=f + best_a*delta
   if np.any(h<0): h=np.maximum(h,0)
   h, _=scale_to_m1(h)
   s=fast_score(h)
   if s < cur*(1-1e-10): return h,s
   return None,cur
```

def random_candidate(f,rng):
   n=f.size
   logf=np.log(np.maximum(f,1e-300))
   r=rng.random()
   if r < 0.5:
       width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//50)))
       start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
       logf[start:start+width] += 0.2*rng.normal(size=width)
   elif r < 0.8:
       width = max(8, min(n, int(n//10)))
       start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
       t = np.linspace(0.0, np.pi, width)
       logf[start:start+width] += 0.15*rng.normal() * np.sin(t)
   else:
       return 0.5*(f + f[::-1])
   cand=np.exp(logf)
   if rng.random() < 0.2:
       cand=0.5*(cand+cand[::-1])
   cand=np.maximum(cand,0)
   return scale_to_m1(cand)[0]
```

```
def line_search(f, g, deadline):
    delta = g - f
    cur = fast_score(f)
    alphas = (0.01,0.02,0.04,0.08,0.15,0.25,0.4,0.6,0.8,1.0)
    best_a = 0.0; best_s = cur
    prev_a = 0.0
    for a in alphas:
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       s = direction_score(f, delta, a)
       if s < best_s:
           best_s=s; best_a=a
       prev_a=a
    if best_a == 0.0:
       return None, cur
    # determine interval
    if best_a <= 0.02:
       left=0.0; right=min(0.2, 1.0)
    elif best_a >= 1.0:
       left=0.7; right=1.0
    else:
       left=0.0; right=1.0
       found_prev=-1
       for i,a in enumerate(alphas):
           if abs(a - best_a) < 1e-12:
              if i>0: left=alphas[i-1]
              right=alphas[i]
              break
       if right <= left:
          left=best_a*0.5
          right=best_a*1.5
          if left<0: left=0
          if right>1: right=1
    # ternary 25
    l=left; r=right
    if r-l > 1e-5:
       for _ in range(25):
          m1 = l + (r-l)*(np.sqrt(5)-1)/2? Ternary easier:
          m1 = l + (r-l)/3.0
          m2 = r - (r-l)/3.0
          s1=direction_score(...); s2=...
          if time >= deadline break
          if s1 < s2: r=m2
          else: l=m1
          if s1 < best_s: best_s=s1; best_a=m1
          if s2 < best_s: best_s=s2; best_a=m2
    h = f + best_a*delta
    if np.any(h<0): h=np.maximum(h,0)
    h, _ = scale_to_m1(h)
    s = fast_score(h)
    if s < cur*(1-1e-10):
       return h, s
    return None, cur
```

def sanitize_list(seq):
   out=[]
   for v in seq:
      vf=float(v)
      if not np.isfinite(vf): vf=0.0
      if vf <0: vf=0.0
      elif vf>1000: vf=1000.0
      out.append(vf)
   return out

```
def optimize_length(n, deadline, start=None):
    if n < 2: return None, np.inf
    if start is not None:
       start_arr = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64)
       if start_arr.size != n:
           # pad/truncate? If start len not n, skip
           f = make_prefix(n)
       else:
           f = np.maximum(start_arr, 0.0)
    else: f=make_prefix(n)
    f, _ = scale_to_m1(f)
    best_f=f.copy(); best_score=fast_score(f)
    print(...)
    pattern=None
    if HAVE_SCIPY and n <= LP_NMAX:
       try: pattern=get_conv_pattern(n)
       except Exception: pattern=None
    rng = np.random.default_rng(12345 + n)
    no_improve=0; it=0; lp_fail=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if best_score <= TARGET_SCORE: break
       if pattern is not None:
           rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
           if rem > 15.0:
              # sample if not too many consecutive fails
              time_limit = min(60.0, max(2.0, rem - 10.0))
              g = solve_lp(f, pattern, time_limit)
              if g is None:
                 lp_fail +=1
                 if lp_fail >=3:
                    release_pattern(n); pattern=None; continue
              else:
                 lp_fail=0
              if g is not None:
                 sg = float(np.sum(g)); sf = float(np.sum(f))
                 if sg > sf*(1+1e-8):
                    search_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 45.0)
                    newf, news = line_search(f, g, search_deadline)
                    if newf is not None and news < best_score*(1 - 1e-10):
                       best_f = newf.copy(); best_score = news; f = newf
                       it +=1; no_improve=0
                       if it % 10 ==0: print(...)
                       continue
       # random fallback
       # to avoid extremely long random loop, track iterations
       cand = random_candidate(f, rng)
       newf, news = blend_search(f, cand, min(deadline, time.monotonic()+5.0))
       if newf is not None and news < best_score*(1 - 1e-10):
          best_f=newf.copy(); best_score=news; f=newf; no_improve=0; it+=1
       else:
          sym, _ = scale_to_m1(0.5*(f + f[::-1]))
          syms = fast_score(sym)
          if syms < best_score*(1 - 1e-10):
             best_f=sym.copy(); best_score=syms; f=sym; no_improve=0
          else:
             no_improve +=1
             if no_improve > 500:
                break
    return best_f, best_score
```
If pattern is None from start (no scipy), no_improve increments random candidates; 500 random attempts then break. For n=1024, random maybe cheap; but breaking after 500 might waste allocation? If no improvement, better stop and move to next length. Good. If we want more random, increase 2000. Use 300.

`blend_search` deadline of 5 seconds uses maybe 10 FFTs; okay. 300* maybe too much? For n=1024, 5s each gives 1500s, but break after300 impossible in 1700? Actually random fallback if LP unavailable: 300* up to5s could >1000s. But blend_search usually early eval 5 alphas ~0.02s each for n=1024, <0.15s. So 300~45s. okay.

In LP iterations, after each success continue, no random. If many successes, good. If LP direction no sum, random.

Potential issue: `line_search` returns h normalized, but `news` from fast_score(h). If `news` improvement very small due safety scale, maybe not update. Good.

Now, pool exact final: The candidate pool may contain sequences with fast score better but exact inf? skip. If all inf, fallback. Good.

Let's include exact evaluation of prefix? Not until final. If search takes all time, final prefix exact. Good.

Now, final code output. Ensure docstring no trailing weird.

One more check: In `get_conv_pattern`, total=n*n for n=2048 => 4194304 fits int32. `indptr` type int32; `len` total 4M fits. SciPy accepts int32 indices? It may want int32 or int64; int32 okay. If n=4096 would overflow? We don't pattern for >2048. Good.

In `solve_lp`, `bounds=[(0.0, None)] * n` no issue with `<`? Each tuple distinct? It's same tuple object but immutable. okay.

`linprog` with `A_ub` csr and `b_ub` list; `b_ub` can be numpy ones. Good.

`res.status`: 0 optimal, 1 infeasible, 2 unbounded, 3 iteration limit, 4 numerical. If 3 maybe res.x None. Return None.

If `linprog` time_limit option causes `res.status=3` but x approximate? We ignore. Could use if x finite. Maybe if res.x is not None and finite, use even status 3? Add:
```
x = getattr(res,'x',None)
if x is not None: ...
```
But if status 3 partial may not satisfy; use as direction maybe okay. To be safe, only status 0 or 3 with finite? Could accept 3 if all finite and f*g approx≤? But time limit rare. Use status 0.

Potential issue: LP `A` matrix with very small f values can cause constraints effectively zero? f prefix min ~0.008 for n=2048, okay. If f has zeros due underflow? no.

Now, let's consider if `np.convolve` in exact evaluation of prefix length 4096 will be slow (16M multiply) but okay. The evaluate_sequence first checks list each element O(n), then int/float otests, then np.convolve. 4096 fine.

Now, if the environment's `evaluate_sequence` is not imported in the namespace where `propose_candidate` runs? It says assume it will already be imported and can be directly invoked. Good.

Let's produce final code. Ensure it begins ```python and ends ```. No analysis in final.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing

    2*n*max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a)**2).

The main constructive starting point is the finite prefix of the infinite
sequence

    a_k = binom(2k, k) / 4^k,  k = 0, 1, ...,

whose square has its first n coefficients equal to 1.  This already gives a
score close to pi/2, a large improvement over 2.  The search then refines the
sequence with a projected-linearizing LP step:

    max sum(g)
    s.t.  f*g <= 1,  g >= 0,

where f is the current sequence normalized so max(f*f) ~ 1.  The LP direction
increases the l1 mass without increasing the active convolution peak.  A line
search follows this direction, and scalar/grid refinements, symmetrization,
and random perturbations are used to avoid stagnation.  The best exact-evaluated
sequence found before the time limit is returned.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

TARGET_SCORE = 1.502700
LP_NMAX = 2048
NORM_DIRECT_N = 1024
MAX_FINAL_LENGTH = 8192
TC = float((np.sqrt(5.0) - 1.0) * 0.5)

_PATTERN_CACHE = {}
_CANDIDATE_POOL = []


def release_pattern(n):
    _PATTERN_CACHE.pop(n, None)


def next_pow2(x):
    r = 1
    while r < int(x):
        r <<= 1
    return r


def conv_max_direct(a):
    return float(np.max(np.convolve(a, a)))


def conv_max_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0
    if n == 1:
        return float(a[0] * a[0])
    if n <= 256:
        return conv_max_direct(a)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    nfft = next_pow2(m)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, nfft)
    val = float(np.max(c[:m]))
    if val < 0.0:
        return 0.0
    return val


def conv_max_best(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return 0.0
    if a.size <= NORM_DIRECT_N:
        return conv_max_direct(a)
    return conv_max_fast(a)


def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    m = conv_max_fast(a)
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def exact_score_list(lst):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def sanitize_list(seq):
    out = []
    for v in seq:
        try:
            x = float(v)
        except Exception:
            x = 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(x):
            x = 0.0
        if x < 0.0:
            x = 0.0
        elif x > 1000.0:
            x = 1000.0
        out.append(x)
    return out


def make_prefix(n):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    k = np.arange(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    ratios = (2.0 * k + 1.0) / (2.0 * k + 2.0)
    tail = np.cumprod(ratios)
    out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    out[0] = 1.0
    out[1:] = tail
    return out


def scale_to_m1(a, safety=1.0e-9):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a, 0.0
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return a, 0.0
    m = conv_max_best(a)
    if m <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(m):
        return a, 0.0
    scale = (1.0 - safety) / np.sqrt(m)
    return a * scale, m


def direction_score(f, delta, alpha):
    h = f + alpha * delta
    if np.any(h < 0.0):
        h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(h))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.inf
    m = conv_max_fast(h)
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * h.size * m / (s * s))


def line_search(f, g, deadline):
    delta = g - f
    cur = fast_score(f)
    alphas = (0.01, 0.02, 0.04, 0.08, 0.15, 0.25, 0.40, 0.60, 0.80, 1.00)

    best_a = 0.0
    best_s = cur

    for a in alphas:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        s = direction_score(f, delta, a)
        if s < best_s:
            best_s = s
            best_a = a

    if best_a <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(best_s):
        return None, cur

    if best_a <= 0.02:
        left = 0.0
        right = min(0.20, 1.0)
    elif best_a >= 1.0:
        left = 0.70
        right = 1.0
    else:
        left = 0.0
        right = 1.0
        found = False
        for i, a in enumerate(alphas):
            if abs(a - best_a) < 1.0e-12:
                if i > 0:
                    left = alphas[i - 1]
                right = alphas[i]
                found = True
                break
        if not found or right <= left:
            left = max(0.0, best_a * 0.5)
            right = min(1.0, best_a * 1.5)
            if right <= left:
                right = min(1.0, left + 0.05)

    if right - left > 1.0e-5:
        l = left
        r = right
        for _ in range(28):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            m1 = l + (r - l) / 3.0
            m2 = r - (r - l) / 3.0
            if abs(m2 - m1) < 1.0e-12:
                break
            s1 = direction_score(f, delta, m1)
            s2 = direction_score(f, delta, m2)
            if s1 < s2:
                r = m2
                if s1 < best_s:
                    best_s = s1
                    best_a = m1
            else:
                l = m1
                if s2 < best_s:
                    best_s = s2
                    best_a = m2

    h = f + best_a * delta
    if np.any(h < 0.0):
        h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
    h, _ = scale_to_m1(h)
    s = fast_score(h)
    if s < cur * (1.0 - 1.0e-10):
        return h, s
    return None, cur


def get_conv_pattern(n):
    if n in _PATTERN_CACHE:
        return _PATTERN_CACHE[n]

    m = 2 * n - 1
    total = n * n
    col = np.empty(total, dtype=np.int32)
    fidx = np.empty(total, dtype=np.int32)
    indptr = np.empty(m + 1, dtype=np.int32)

    pos = 0
    for k in range(m):
        indptr[k] = pos
        start = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        end = min(n - 1, k)
        cnt = end - start + 1
        if cnt > 0:
            col[pos:pos + cnt] = np.arange(start, end + 1, dtype=np.int32)
            fidx[pos:pos + cnt] = np.arange(k, k - cnt, -1, dtype=np.int32)
        pos += cnt
    indptr[m] = pos

    pat = {
        "n": int(n),
        "m": int(m),
        "col": col,
        "fidx": fidx,
        "indptr": indptr,
    }
    if n <= 1024:
        _PATTERN_CACHE[n] = pat
    return pat


def solve_lp(f, pat, time_limit):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    n = f.size
    try:
        data = f[pat["fidx"]].astype(np.float64, copy=False)
        A = csr_matrix((data, pat["col"], pat["indptr"]), shape=(pat["m"], n))
        c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.ones(pat["m"], dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        options = {
            "presolve": True,
            "time_limit": float(max(1.0, time_limit)),
        }
        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options=options,
        )
        if res is None:
            return None
        if res.status != 0:
            return None
        x = getattr(res, "x", None)
        if x is None:
            return None
        g = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
        if g.size != n:
            return None
        g = np.where(g > 0.0, g, 0.0)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            return None
        return g
    except Exception:
        return None


def random_candidate(f, rng):
    n = f.size
    logf = np.log(np.maximum(f, 1.0e-300))
    r = rng.random()

    if r < 0.55:
        max_width = max(4, n // 20)
        width = int(rng.integers(1, max_width + 1))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        logf[start:start + width] += 0.18 * rng.normal(size=width)
    elif r < 0.80:
        width = max(8, min(n, int(n // 10)))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        t = np.linspace(0.0, np.pi, width)
        amp = 0.12 * rng.normal()
        logf[start:start + width] += amp * np.sin(t)
    elif r < 0.92:
        width = max(2, min(n, int(n // 50)))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        logf[start:start + width] += 0.35 * rng.normal(size=width)
    else:
        return 0.5 * (f + f[::-1])

    cand = np.exp(logf)
    if rng.random() < 0.25:
        cand = 0.5 * (cand + cand[::-1])
    cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
    cand, _ = scale_to_m1(cand)
    return cand


def blend_search(f, cand, deadline):
    cur = fast_score(f)
    delta = cand - f
    alphas = (0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00)
    best_a = 0.0
    best_s = cur

    for a in alphas:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        s = direction_score(f, delta, a)
        if s < best_s:
            best_s = s
            best_a = a

    if best_a <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(best_s):
        return None, cur

    h = f + best_a * delta
    if np.any(h < 0.0):
        h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
    h, _ = scale_to_m1(h)
    s = fast_score(h)
    if s < cur * (1.0 - 1.0e-10):
        return h, s
    return None, cur


def add_candidate(seq, est_score):
    if seq is None:
        return
    arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return
    if arr.size > MAX_FINAL_LENGTH:
        return
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        return
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if s < 0.01:
        return
    _CANDIDATE_POOL.append([float(est_score), arr.copy()])
    if len(_CANDIDATE_POOL) > 10:
        worst_i = 0
        worst_v = -np.inf
        for i, item in enumerate(_CANDIDATE_POOL):
            if item[0] > worst_v:
                worst_v = item[0]
                worst_i = i
        _CANDIDATE_POOL.pop(worst_i)


def optimize_length(n, deadline, start=None):
    if n < 2:
        return None, np.inf

    if start is not None:
        st = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64)
        if st.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(st)) and float(np.sum(np.maximum(st, 0.0))) > 0.0:
            f = np.maximum(st, 0.0)
        else:
            f = make_prefix(n)
        f, _ = scale_to_m1(f)
    else:
        f = make_prefix(n)
        f, _ = scale_to_m1(f)

    best_f = f.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(f)
    print(f"optimize n={n}: initial fast score={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)

    if best_score <= np.inf and best_score >= 0.0:
        add_candidate(best_f, best_score)

    pattern = None
    if HAVE_SCIPY and n <= LP_NMAX:
        try:
            pattern = get_conv_pattern(n)
        except Exception:
            pattern = None

    rng = np.random.default_rng(12345 + n)
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    lp_fail = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if best_score <= TARGET_SCORE:
            break

        if pattern is not None:
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem > 18.0:
                time_limit = min(60.0, max(2.0, rem - 12.0))
                g = solve_lp(f, pattern, time_limit)
                if g is None:
                    lp_fail += 1
                    if lp_fail >= 4:
                        release_pattern(n)
                        pattern = None
                        continue
                else:
                    lp_fail = 0

                sg = float(np.sum(g))
                sf = float(np.sum(f))
                if sg > sf * (1.0 + 1.0e-8) and np.isfinite(sg):
                    search_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 45.0)
                    newf, news = line_search(f, g, search_deadline)
                    if newf is not None and np.isfinite(news) and news < best_score * (1.0 - 1.0e-10):
                        best_f = newf.copy()
                        best_score = news
                        f = newf
                        it += 1
                        no_improve = 0
                        add_candidate(best_f, best_score)
                        if it % 10 == 0:
                            print(f"n={n} iter={it} fast score={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)
                        continue

        cand = random_candidate(f, rng)
        search_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 5.0)
        newf, news = blend_search(f, cand, search_deadline)

        if newf is not None and np.isfinite(news) and news < best_score * (1.0 - 1.0e-10):
            best_f = newf.copy()
            best_score = news
            f = newf
            no_improve = 0
            it += 1
            add_candidate(best_f, best_score)
            if it % 50 == 0:
                print(f"n={n} random-iter={it} fast score={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)
        else:
            sym, _ = scale_to_m1(0.5 * (f + f[::-1]))
            syms = fast_score(sym)
            if np.isfinite(syms) and syms < best_score * (1.0 - 1.0e-10):
                best_f = sym.copy()
                best_score = syms
                f = sym
                no_improve = 0
                add_candidate(best_f, best_score)
            else:
                no_improve += 1
                if no_improve > 400:
                    break

    return best_f, best_score


def propose_candidate(**kwargs):
    deadline = time.monotonic() + 1770.0

    _CANDIDATE_POOL.clear()
    _PATTERN_CACHE.clear()

    # If a known sequence is available, evaluate it immediately and keep it as a candidate.
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1
        if h is not None:
            hig = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64)
            if hig.ndim == 1 and 0 < hig.size <= MAX_FINAL_LENGTH and np.all(np.isfinite(hig)):
                hig = np.maximum(hig, 0.0)
                if float(np.sum(hig)) >= 0.01:
                    ex = exact_score_list(sanitize_list(hig))
                    print(f"height_sequence_1 length={hig.size} exact score={ex:.7f}", flush=True)
                    add_candidate(hig, ex if np.isfinite(ex) else fast_score(hig))
                    if np.isfinite(ex) and ex <= TARGET_SCORE:
                        return sanitize_list(hig)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Strong analytic starters: prefixes of central-binomial coefficients.
    for n0 in (512, 1024, 2048, 4096):
        p = make_prefix(n0)
        p, _ = scale_to_m1(p)
        est = fast_score(p)
        add_candidate(p, est)
        print(f"prefix n={n0} fast score={est:.7f}", flush=True)

    if HAVE_SCIPY:
        schedule = (
            (512, 240),
            (1024, 820),
            (2048, 520),
        )
    else:
        schedule = (
            (1024, 1700),
        )

    for n0, alloc in schedule:
        tnow = time.monotonic()
        if tnow + 30.0 > deadline:
            break
        local_deadline = min(deadline - 25.0, tnow + float(alloc))
        if local_deadline <= time.monotonic() + 10.0:
            continue

        print(f"starting LP/refinement search for n={n0}, allocated {alloc}s", flush=True)
        seq, est = optimize_length(n0, local_deadline)
        if seq is not None:
            add_candidate(seq, est)
            # Exact-check the best from this length to preserve the official value.
            lst = sanitize_list(seq)
            ex = exact_score_list(lst)
            print(f"finished n={n0}: exact best={ex:.7f}", flush=True)
            if np.isfinite(ex) and ex <= TARGET_SCORE:
                return lst

    # Final authoritative selection from the candidate pool.
    best_seq = None
    best_score = np.inf

    for item in _CANDIDATE_POOL:
        arr = np.asarray(item[1], dtype=np.float64)
        if arr.size < 1 or arr.size > MAX_FINAL_LENGTH:
            continue
        lst = sanitize_list(arr)
        sc = exact_score_list(lst)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = lst

    if best_seq is None:
        base = make_prefix(4096)
        base, _ = scale_to_m1(base)
        best_seq = sanitize_list(base)
        best_score = exact_score_list(best_seq)

    print(f"final returned length={len(best_seq)} exact score={best_score:.7f}", flush=True)
    return best_seq
```
